==================================================

Create `allItems` as a derived flattened index from:

- model groups
- products
- characters
- community
- apps
- Originals

Do not manually maintain a separate allItems list.

Likewise derive:

appItems

from products where appropriate.

==================================================
DO NOT BUILD
==================================================

- Algolia
- fuzzy search
- semantic search
- embeddings

timestamp: 2026-09-17T14:29:19.766Z
model: gpt-5.6-sol-low

you added similar eslint enforcemnt? Here’s how this repo handles it:

### Rule (convention)
Documented in `README.Rules.md`:

**Sub-features cannot import from sibling sub-features.**  
If something is shared, **lift it to the parent feature** (`components/`, `hooks/`, `services/`, `store/`, `models/`, etc.) with its dependencies.

Also:
- Features can import from other features’ top-level surface (components/hooks/models), **not** from another feature’s `sub-features/`
- Modules must not import from `features/` (encapsulation)

Same idea in `README.Mfe.md` as a checklist: no imports from sibling features.

---

### ESLint enforcement (what actually warns/errors)
In `.eslintrc.json`, the automated boundary is:

```63:73:.eslintrc.json
    "import/no-restricted-paths": [
      "error",
      {
        "zones": [
          {
            "target": "apps/client-portal/src/modules/",
            "from": "apps/client-portal/src/features/",
            "message": "Can not Import any thing from features. Modules should be standalone"
          }
        ]
      }
    ],
```

That’s **modules → features**, not a full “no sibling sub-feature” zone.

`no-restricted-imports` here only blocks certain MUI deep imports — not sibling folders.

---

### Practical takeaway
| Boundary | How it’s enforced |
|----------|-------------------|
| Sub-feature ↔ sibling sub-feature | Convention (`README.Rules.md`) — lift shared code up |
| Feature ↔ sibling feature (deep/sub-feature) | Convention + MFE checklist |
| Module → Feature | **ESLint** `import/no-restricted-paths` (error) |

So: sibling-folder avoidance is mainly a **folder/architecture rule**; the lint config that “gives a warning” today is mainly the **modules must not import features** zone. Sibling sub-feature imports are not fully blocked by ESLint yet.

There is a serious visual flickering issue in the Home Higgsfield Genjutsu section.

When hovering ANY Genjutsu preset card, unrelated UI including the global header/nav visibly flickers.

I inspected the current implementation and this looks like a browser GPU/compositing issue, not Header state.

Fix this narrowly. Do NOT redesign Genjutsu.

==================================================
LIKELY CAUSES IN CURRENT CODE
==================================================

Inspect:

src/features/home/genjutsu/GenjutsuPresetCard.tsx
src/features/home/genjutsu/GenjutsuSection.tsx

Current GenjutsuPresetCard uses:

INTERSECT_MARGIN = "600px 0px"

and effectively:

const playing = active || focused

This means many cards around the viewport mount and play MP4 video elements simultaneously.

The desktop Genjutsu section also currently forces the entire large framed section into a composited layer using:

[transform:translateZ(0)]

and NewModelBadge contains:

backdrop-blur-lg
-webkit-backdrop-filter: blur(...)

Then card hover changes multiple overlay opacities.

This combination can cause expensive/full-page GPU recompositing and explains why even the Header flickers.

==================================================
FIX PRIORITY 1 — DO NOT PLAY EVERY CARD
==================================================

On desktop, Genjutsu videos should only mount/play for the card the user is actually interacting with.

Change the playback condition.

Conceptually:

const playing =
  chrome === "desktop"
    ? active && (hovered || focused)
    : active || focused

Meaning:

DESKTOP:
- poster by default
- entering/focusing card starts that ONE card's video
- leaving card removes/pauses video
- offscreen cards never play

MOBILE:
- retain current in-view behavior if needed because mobile has no hover

The important requirement is:

DO NOT have the whole desktop Genjutsu masonry playing videos simultaneously.

Keep IntersectionObserver as an offscreen guard.

Also reduce the excessive observer preload margin.

Change:

600px 0px

to something conservative, approximately:

150px 0px
or
200px 0px

Do not preload/play cards several screens away.

==================================================
FIX PRIORITY 2 — REMOVE SECTION-WIDE GPU FORCING
==================================================

In GenjutsuDesktopSection remove:

[transform:translateZ(0)]

from the OUTER Genjutsu section.

Do not force the entire large section into one GPU compositing layer.

Keep:

relative
isolate
overflow-hidden
rounded-3xl

etc.

Do NOT replace it with:

transform-gpu
will-change-transform
will-change-opacity

on the whole section.

That would make the issue worse.

==================================================
FIX PRIORITY 3 — KEEP VIDEO COMPOSITING LOCAL
==================================================

If the playing video itself needs GPU isolation, keep it local to the media element/card only.

For example it is okay for the individual media/card to use:

backface-visibility: hidden

or, only if actually required:

transform: translateZ(0)

But do not apply compositing hacks to:

- the whole Genjutsu section
- Home
- AppShell
- Header

The composited area should be as small as possible.

==================================================
FIX PRIORITY 4 — HOVER STATE
==================================================

Current card uses React:

hovered
setHovered(true / false)

and also CSS:

group-hover/explore-card

Keep React hovered state ONLY because desktop video playback now depends on it.

Do not introduce parent-level hover state.

Hovering one card must cause only that card to rerender.

Do NOT store hovered card ID in:

- Zustand
- GenjutsuSection
- Home
- global state

==================================================
FIX PRIORITY 5 — BACKDROP FILTER
==================================================

After the first fixes, test the page.

If flickering still exists, inspect NewModelBadge in GenjutsuSection.

It currently uses backdrop blur.

Because it sits inside a section containing videos, Safari/Chrome GPU composition can behave badly with:

video + backdrop-filter + transforms

If flicker remains, remove:

backdrop-blur-lg
[-webkit-backdrop-filter:blur(1rem)]

from NewModelBadge.

Keep the visual appearance using its existing translucent green background and inset shadow.

Do NOT remove blur globally from other components.

Only change this badge if necessary.

==================================================
PROGRESS UPDATES
==================================================

Genjutsu videos call onTimeUpdate and write:

--explore-video-progress

to actionsRef.

This is fine for ONE actively hovered video.

Make sure these time updates are NOT running across 10–20 simultaneously playing Genjutsu cards.

With hover-only desktop playback this should naturally be resolved.

Do not move playback progress into React state.

Keep the direct CSS variable write.

==================================================
VIDEO LIFECYCLE
==================================================

Preserve:

- poster before video starts
- muted
- loop
- playsInline
- disablePictureInPicture
- object-cover

When hover/focus ends:

- pause/unmount video cleanly
- poster becomes visible again

Avoid flashing between poster/video.

The poster and video should occupy identical bounds.

Do not alter card dimensions when the video mounts.

==================================================
IMPORTANT: NO LAYOUT SHIFT
==================================================

Hovering a Genjutsu card must NOT change:

- card width
- card height
- masonry column dimensions
- section height
- scrollbar presence
- Header dimensions

Only overlays and media playback should change.

==================================================
TEST THE EXACT BUG
==================================================

Run the app in Chrome desktop.

Go to Home → Higgsfield Genjutsu.

Before hovering:

- inspect how many `<video>` nodes exist/play inside Genjutsu
- there should NOT be 20 actively playing videos

leave

4. New model badge does not flicker

==================================================

- Recreate still works

==================================================
DO NOT
==================================================

Do NOT attempt to fix this by adding:

to everything.

to Header/AppShell/Home.

Do NOT memoize the whole application.

Do NOT change Header code unless profiling actually proves Header itself is triggering rerenders.

We found a separate navigation bug in the current repo.

Fix SPA navigation/remounting for all ROUTES THAT OUR REBUILD ACTUALLY OWNS.

Do not do a broad refactor.

==================================================
CONFIRMED ROOT CAUSE
==================================================

Inspect:

src/components/header/HeaderNav.tsx
src/components/header/HeaderMegaMenu.tsx

Current `NavHref` effectively does:

if (href === "/") {
  return <Link to="/" ... />
}

return <a href={href} ... />

This means:

Explore → Image
Explore → Video
Genjutsu
Image model links
Video model links

use normal browser `<a href>` navigation.

That causes a FULL DOCUMENT RELOAD:

old React app destroyed
→ Vite document loaded again
→ React root recreated
→ AppShell recreated
→ Header recreated
→ route recreated

This is why navigation visually looks like the whole UI rerenders/flashes.

This is NOT just a normal React rerender.

HeaderMegaMenu also currently renders rows using:

<a href={item.href}>

so model selection from Header mega menus has the same problem.

==================================================
TARGET BEHAVIOR
==================================================

Internal navigation for routes implemented by our SPA must stay client-side.

For example:

/ 
→ /ai/image?model=gpt_image_2

must NOT reload the document.

/ai/image?model=gpt_image_2
→ /ai/image?model=soul-v2

must NOT reload the document.

/ 
→ /ai/video

must NOT reload the document.

/ 
→ /ai/video?model=genjutsu

must NOT reload the document.

/ai/video
→ /ai/video/motion

must NOT reload the document.

Shared components should stay mounted:

AppShell
Header
PromotionHeader
AuthModal
UpgradeModal
SearchModal

Only route Outlet content should change.

==================================================
IMPORTANT DISTINCTION
==================================================

A Header RENDER on navigation is okay.

HeaderNav subscribes to:

pathname
search

because it needs to update active nav state.

Therefore this is acceptable:

Header render
→ active item updates

This is NOT acceptable:

Header unmount
Header mount

or:

window/document reload
favicon/page flash
promo state resetting
all assets reconstructing

Do NOT try to eliminate every Header render.

Fix DOCUMENT NAVIGATION / REMOUNTING.

==================================================
ROUTES CURRENTLY OWNED BY THE SPA
==================================================

Inspect the current TanStack route tree instead of guessing.

At minimum the current repo contains:

/
 /ai/image

The previous Genjutsu flicker fix changed the playback behavior incorrectly.

I reviewed the current Genjutsu files.

IMPORTANT PRODUCT REQUIREMENT:

Genjutsu videos should ALWAYS PLAY while their card is in the viewport.

Hover must NOT control video playback.

Hover should ONLY reveal card chrome:
- mode label
- media selector
- Recreate button

Fix the current implementation narrowly.

==================================================
CONFIRMED CURRENT BUG
==================================================

In:

src/features/home/genjutsu/GenjutsuPresetCard.tsx

current code effectively does:

const playing =
  chrome === "desktop"
    ? active && (hovered || focused)
    : active || focused;

This is wrong.

It means desktop video playback starts on mouse enter and stops on mouse leave.

Because the `<video>` itself is conditionally rendered using `playing`, hover currently causes:

hover
→ React rerender
→ video DOM mounted
→ video begins loading/playing
→ onPlaying
→ presenting changes
→ poster disappears

mouseleave
→ React rerender
→ video DOM removed
→ poster comes back

This causes the strange visual transition/flicker we now see.

DO NOT make hover control media playback.

==================================================
CORRECT BEHAVIOR
==================================================

Playback should depend ONLY on viewport visibility.

Conceptually:

const playing = active && !reducedMotion;

or equivalent.

DESKTOP:

card enters viewport
→ video starts

card remains visible
→ video keeps playing continuously

hover card
→ video continues uninterrupted
→ only card controls fade in

leave hover
→ video continues uninterrupted

card leaves viewport
→ video pauses/unmounts

MOBILE:

same visibility-driven behavior.

Hover/focus has nothing to do with whether the video plays.

==================================================
SIMPLIFY INTERSECTION OBSERVER
==================================================

Current implementation has:

INTERSECT_MARGIN = "150px 0px"
LEAVE_DELAY_MS = 500

plus:
- timeout
- requestAnimationFrame
- local visible variable

This is unnecessarily complicated for the desired behavior.

We want:

VISIBLE IN VIEWPORT = PLAY
NOT VISIBLE = DON'T PLAY

Use a normal IntersectionObserver.

Recommended:

rootMargin: "0px"
threshold: 0.01

Conceptually:

function useExploreActive() {
  const [node, setNode] = useState<HTMLElement | null>(null);
  const [active, setActive] = useState(false);

  useEffect(() => {
    if (!node) return;

    const observer = new IntersectionObserver(
      ([entry]) => {
        setActive(Boolean(entry?.isIntersecting));
      },
      {
        rootMargin: "0px",
        threshold: 0.01,
      },
    );

    observer.observe(node);

    return () => observer.disconnect();
  }, [node]);

  return { setNode, active };
}

Do not keep the 500ms leave timer unless there is a proven visual reason.

Do not use a large preload margin.

The visible cards themselves should play.

==================================================
REMOVE REACT HOVER STATE
==================================================

Current GenjutsuPresetCard has:

const [hovered, setHovered] = useState(false);

and:

onMouseEnter={() => setHovered(true)}
onMouseLeave={() => setHovered(false)}

This is unnecessary.

The controls already use Tailwind/CSS:

group-hover/explore-card
group-focus-within/explore-card

Use CSS for hover presentation.

Remove:

hovered state

data-hovered

onMouseEnter
onMouseLeave

and remove:

group-data-[hovered]/explore-card:...

from control classes.

Keep:

group-hover/explore-card:opacity-100
group-focus-within/explore-card:opacity-100

This means hovering a card no longer triggers React state updates.

==================================================
REMOVE REACT FOCUS STATE IF IT ONLY EXISTS FOR PLAYBACK
==================================================

Current component also has:

const [focused, setFocused] = useState(false);

and focus capture handlers.

Since playback will no longer depend on focus, determine whether this state has any other real purpose.

The UI already uses:

group-focus-within/explore-card

for focus-visible controls.

If `focused` is only used by the old `playing` expression:

REMOVE:
- focused state
- onFocusCapture
- onBlurCapture

Let CSS `group-focus-within` handle control visibility.

Do not remove keyboard accessibility.

The selector buttons and Recreate link remain naturally focusable.

==================================================
PLAYBACK
==================================================

Desired media logic:

const playing = active;

Then:

{playing ? (
  <video ... />
) : null}

Keep:

loop
muted
playsInline
disablePictureInPicture
poster={media.posterSrc}

When media.id changes while card is visible:
→ new selected media starts playing automatically.

Current:

key={media.id}

is acceptable for switching selected videos.

==================================================
PREFERS REDUCED MOTION
==================================================

Respect reduced motion.

If user prefers reduced motion:

- show poster
- do not autoplay video

Ideally avoid mounting/loading the video unnecessarily.

Use an existing reduced-motion hook if the project has one.

Otherwise a tiny local hook is acceptable.

Do NOT repeatedly call:

window.matchMedia(...)

inside arbitrary render/effect paths if a reusable project primitive already exists.

Conceptually:

const shouldPlay = active && !reducedMotion;

==================================================
POSTER / VIDEO TRANSITION
==================================================

Keep the poster underneath/above the video so there is no blank frame during initial load.

Current `presenting` state is acceptable:

poster visible
→ video mounts
→ video actually starts
→ onPlaying sets presenting=true
→ poster hides

However:

hover must NEVER change `presenting`.

Only actual video playback events should.

When a visible card is hovered, poster/video state must remain completely unchanged.

==================================================
IMPORTANT: VIDEO MUST NOT RESTART ON HOVER
==================================================

Test this explicitly.

Hover a playing card.

Record:

video.currentTime

Move mouse out.

Move mouse back in.

The same video should still be progressing normally.

Hovering must not:

- remount video
- reset currentTime
- reload src
- flash poster
- restart playback

==================================================
HOVER UI
==================================================

Hover should ONLY do this:

mode chip:
opacity 0 → 1

bottom actions:
opacity 0 → 1

Nothing else.

No:
- scaling card
- scaling video
- brightness changes on main media
- poster transition
- video transition
- transforms
- blur
- layout shift

Keep card dimensions perfectly stable.

The media selector thumbnail itself currently uses:

hover:brightness-150

That is okay if visually intentional, but do not apply brightness/filter effects to the MAIN CARD MEDIA.

If the strange effect remains after playback is decoupled from hover, temporarily remove thumbnail hover brightness too and compare.

==================================================
MEDIA SELECTOR
==================================================

Keep GenjutsuMediaSelector behavior.

The selector domain is correct:

lead = edit/showcase
then source
then remaining variants

Do not rewrite this.

Current data has real source/edit/generation variants and should remain unchanged.

When user selects a thumbnail:

setSelectedId(id)

If card is visible:
→ selected video should load and play

If card is outside viewport:
→ only its poster should be shown

The selected thumbnail progress ring should continue updating from the playing video.

==================================================
PROGRESS UPDATE
==================================================

Keep:

onTimeUpdate
→ direct CSS custom property
--explore-video-progress

Do NOT move playback progress into React state.

The current direct DOM style write is good because it avoids rerendering the card continuously.

==================================================
DO NOT CHANGE DATA
==================================================

Do not modify:

genjutsu.data.ts
genjutsu.media.ts

unless required for an actual bug.

The preset data structure is correct.

The data explicitly has:

source
variants
generatedVariants
activeGeneratedVariantId

and the helper functions already correctly resolve showcase media and selector ordering.

This task is about interaction/playback.

==================================================
DO NOT ADD GPU HACKS
==================================================

Do NOT add:

will-change
transform: translateZ(0)
transform-gpu
backface hacks
contain: paint

to the whole section/header/page.

We already saw strange compositing behavior.

Solve the actual playback/hover state issue first.

==================================================
CHECK GENJUTSU SECTION TOO
==================================================

Inspect GenjutsuSection.tsx.

If the previous flicker pass removed a section-level:

transform: translateZ(0)

KEEP IT REMOVED.

Do not restore a forced GPU layer around the full Genjutsu section.

If NewModelBadge backdrop blur was removed during the previous fix:
do not restore it merely for this change.

This task must not reintroduce section-wide compositing hacks.

==================================================
EXPECTED FINAL CARD STATE
==================================================

The component should conceptually be much simpler:

state:
- active / in viewport
- selectedId
- presenting

NOT:
- hovered
- focused
- active
- selectedId
- presenting

Hover and focus presentation belong to CSS.

Viewport visibility belongs to IntersectionObserver.

Playback belongs to viewport visibility.

That separation is the goal.

==================================================
VERIFY IN BROWSER
==================================================

Open Home → Higgsfield Genjutsu.

Test an in-view card WITHOUT hovering.

Expected:

VIDEO IS ALREADY PLAYING.

Then hover it.

Expected:

video keeps playing uninterrupted
mode label appears
media selector appears
Recreate appears

Move mouse away.

Expected:

video keeps playing uninterrupted
controls fade away

Use console temporarily:

const video = document.querySelector(...)

Check currentTime before/after hover.

It must continue increasing.

Then scroll card completely out of viewport.

Expected:

video pauses/unmounts.

Scroll it back in.

Expected:

video starts again.

==================================================
TEST MULTIPLE CARDS
==================================================

If 8 Genjutsu cards are physically visible at once:

all 8 are allowed to play.

That is the desired behavior.

Do NOT artificially limit playback to one hovered card.

Cards outside the viewport must not play.

==================================================
VERIFY NO HOVER REACT RENDER
==================================================

Temporarily add:

console.count(`Genjutsu card render ${preset.id}`);

Hover in/out repeatedly.

After the fix, hover alone should NOT rerender the card.

A render is expected when:
- entering/leaving viewport
- selecting media
- video presenting state changes

But not simple mouse hover.

Remove the debug log afterward.

==================================================
ACCEPTANCE
==================================================

1. video automatically plays when card enters viewport
2. desktop does NOT require hover to play
3. mobile continues in-view autoplay
4. hover never starts/stops video
5. hover never remounts video
6. hover never flashes poster
7. hover only reveals controls
8. leaving hover only hides controls
9. selecting media switches to that video
10. selected video plays when visible
11. progress ring still works
12. reduced motion still shows poster

14. no section-wide translateZ/compositing hacks

18. lint passes

FINAL REPORT
==================================================

Implement, then report only:

[LOG_ENTRY type=RESPONSE num=115 session=e43d6375]
timestamp: 2026-09-17T16:04:12.743Z
model: gpt-5.6-sol-low

Review: the observer is necessary for media lifecycle, not button visibility.

Current behavior:
- Hover controls already use CSS only.
- Hover does not trigger React state.
- IntersectionObserver causes one render when entering/leaving viewport so videos can mount/unmount.
- Media selection causes one required render.
- `onTimeUpdate` writes directly to CSS and does not render.
- `memo` prevents ordinary parent renders when props are stable.

Recommended cleanup:
1. Remove `console.log("card")`.
2. Keep one shared IntersectionObserver; offscreen playback cannot be controlled reliably with CSS.
3. Extract it to `home/hooks/useInViewport.ts`.
4. Add a reactive shared `useReducedMotion` hook instead of calling `matchMedia()` during render.
5. Keep controls permanently rendered and CSS-hidden exactly as they are.
6. Keep conditional video mounting; rendering every video and imperatively pausing it would load more media and create worse debt.
7. Consider extracting the poster/video block into a small memoized `GenjutsuMedia` component for readability—not performance necessity.
8. Retain `memo`; preset records appear stable.

The apparent “many renders” when scrolling into the section are expected: each newly visible card must render once to mount its video. The real unnecessary item is the debug log, not the observer architecture.
